# Decisions

What has been decided, by whom, and why. Claude Code adds build decisions at the bottom (the next ID in its lane, newest last). Anything in **Product decisions** needs the product lead's agreement to change.

**Status:** `Decided` — settled. `Default` — applied until the named owner confirms or overrides. `Open` — see `ARCHITECTURE.md` §11.

## IDs when several sessions build at once (BLD-A161)

**Build decisions carry a lane letter:** `BLD-A161`, `BLD-B230`, `BLD-C001`. Each lane counts on by itself, so lanes never have to agree on a number and there is no ceiling. Lanes A and B carry on from their last plain number. A new lane starts at 001.

**Old IDs stay as they are.** Plain numbers stopped at BLD-160 (lane A) and BLD-229 (lane B). Code, tests and commit messages cite those IDs.

**Product decisions keep one sequence** (DEC-096, DEC-097, and on), because they are the numbers the product lead refers to. Add a DEC row only on `main`, straight after `git pull --rebase`. Commit that file on its own and push at once.

**From a worktree, do this without touching the main checkout,** which other sessions share:

1. Commit your own work first.
2. Run `git fetch origin && git switch --detach origin/main`.
3. Add the row and commit.
4. Run `git push origin HEAD:main`.
5. Run `git switch -` and rebase.

Never run `git reset`, `git stash` or a whole-file `git add` in a checkout another session is using.

**A new session claims a lane** by taking the next free letter and adding a row below on `main` before it writes its first build decision.

**`make lint` checks all of this** (`scripts/ci/check_decision_ids.py`). It fails on any of these:

- an ID used twice;
- a malformed ID;
- an unregistered lane letter;
- a new plain-number build ID.

| Lane | Work | Where it works | Plain numbers it used | Migrations (BLD-A162) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | Lead build: the gates, the demo stack, `main` | the main checkout and short-lived `.worktrees/*` branches | BLD-001 to BLD-160 | `202611211xxxxx` |
| B | Parallel build | `.worktrees/build-b*` | BLD-200 to BLD-229 | `202611212xxxxx` |
| C | Feature revisions and new features | `.worktrees/build-c` | none | `202611213xxxxx` |
| D | Design track: every screen and interaction | `.worktrees/design` | none | `202611214xxxxx` (reserved; it should not need one) |
| E | Planning and the design-handoff cut-over (`docs/SHIP_PLAN_1PC.md`): contracts, the schema cut-over, retirements, the idea page, My ideas, the Wall, Profile | `.worktrees/handoff` | none | `202611215xxxxx` (sorts after every other lane's, so the cut-over runs last on a fresh database) |
| F | The handoff's team-up, crew, conversation and search (`docs/SHIP_PLAN_1PC.md` §5) | `.worktrees/build-f*` | none | `202611226xxxxx` (after the cut-over) |
| G | Journeys: the end-to-end journey suite (`apps/web/journeys/`), journey walks at each checkpoint, breaks sent to the owning lane | `.worktrees/journeys` (branch `g/journeys`) | none | none (writes no migrations) |

**After the handoff cut-over (BLD-E006) every lane uses a range that sorts after it**, because the new tables arrive in the cut-over: A `202611221xxxxx`, B `202611222xxxxx`, C `202611223xxxxx`, D `202611224xxxxx`, E `202611225xxxxx` (the cut-over itself is `20261121500100`), F `202611226xxxxx`. Write no migration until the cut-over is on `main`; until then build against `1pc-handoff/06-contracts/`.

| DEC-004 | 6 Nov 2026 | **Category** is the submitter's call: cost saving, new revenue, both, not sure yet. **Play**: process improvement, upsell, cross-sell, new product, other | Committees sort first on value to KFintech; the submitter knows best | Decided | Product lead |
| DEC-005 | 6 Nov 2026 | The canvas is a **thinking aid**, not a form: plain questions, "why we ask", real examples, strength meter that guides and never blocks, assistant suggests and the person decides | People should ask themselves the right questions and come back to improve ideas | Decided | Product lead |
| DEC-006 | 6 Nov 2026 | **Attachments allowed** (up to 5 research files), plus references as links or notes on whether it is solved elsewhere and KFintech's advantage | Research already done saves weeks later | Decided | Product lead |

## Defaults awaiting confirmation (from ARCHITECTURE "Defaults applied")

| ID | Default | Confirm with |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Provider-neutral gateway; Anthropic API or AWS Bedrock, whichever InfoSec clears; zero data retention; agreed region | InfoSec |
| D2 | KFin private infrastructure, India region, containers | IT, InfoSec |
| D4 | Ideas visible org-wide; submitter can choose "committee only" with a reason | Product |
| D5 | Scorecard and priority weights as in `rules/`; 1% Club = accepted after presenting | Core committee, HR, Finance |
| D6 | Next.js + Postgres 16/pgvector + Python worker | Tech |
| D8 | Blind scoring | Core committee |
| D9 | People view for admins and committees only; each person sees their own | HR |

## Build defaults (Claude Code may revise; record the change below)

| ID | Default | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| BLD-001 | SQL migrations with `dbmate`; Kysely with generated types in TypeScript; psycopg 3 in Python | One schema source for two languages |
| BLD-002 | `LLM_MODE=fake` in tests (outputs from `agents/<name>/examples/`) | Tests never need a key or network |
| BLD-003 | `EMBEDDINGS_MODE=hash` in tests; BGE-M3 in UAT and prod | Fast deterministic tests |
| BLD-004 | Dev services: MinIO, ClamAV, a mock OIDC server, Mailpit | Local stack mirrors production roles |
| BLD-005 | One personal-data pattern file (YAML) loaded by both TypeScript and Python, with shared test vectors | Browser and server must agree |
| BLD-006 | Kill switches cached for 10 seconds | Fast to flip, cheap to check |

| BLD-221 | 10 Nov 2026 | **Area 2 review fixes** (independent review: 0 critical, 8 important, 7 minor). **Conflicts of interest** are checked live on every committee view and action, not only when a round opens. The full chain is not used: the core committee is senior, so a three-level chain would flag its members on most groups (in the synthetic directory the chair sits two levels above an e2e contributor). The "team named in who is affected" check is not used either: that section names kinds of people, not teams, and LOB committees are drawn from the LOB they review. An admin clears a manager flag with a reason on the new **Conflict flags** page (`/admin/conflicts`, linked from Admin). A contributor's own flag is never cleared; they step aside. If the chair has a conflict, an admin grants `core_chair` to a stand-in for that group. **Accepting opens the delivery handover** in the same transaction (lane A's `start_from_pick`, §4.8): build and reuse go to the build track, buy and partner to procurement, and the owner can change it in the pack. **A LOB with no active LOB committee** sends its round to the core committee (the cluster's route becomes `core`), so a group never waits on an empty roster. **Model calls leave the long transactions:** qualify starts in a `qualify.start` job after the approval commits, and an hourly `qualify.backfill` picks up groups approved while `committee.flow` was off or whose start failed. `prescore` and `pitch-prep` also run as jobs (`round.prescore`, `presentation.outline`), and opening a cycle takes an advisory lock. **Contributors read the pitch outline in neutral mode,** since it draws on every idea in the group (non-negotiable 7). **A no-show** gets its own message, `decision.not_presented` (new draft template `not-presented`, for HR review). Assessments close once the sheet is collated, and collation runs once. "Member n" follows a hash order, not submission order. Who offered to sponsor shows to the chair only after collation. Accepting needs a plain reason of at least 30 characters. A pass over the rules' "fail" needs a note. Scores, assessments and votes start unselected on screen. Changing the outcome clears the reason code. A decision on 29 February goes stale on 28 February a year later. Found while running the gates: committee emails failed to render (a local import shadowed `vault`); the decision sheet's score did not serialise; and the name detector read a cluster ID (`CL-0010`) as a person, so its tag reached the judge as a candidate ID and every scan that matched that cluster failed. The name detector now skips a value only when it is exactly one of the platform's own IDs; every other detection is unchanged. The e2e specs that flip `clusters.batch` take turns through a lock, and they find their group proposal by idea ID rather than by its words. | BLD-222 | 10 Nov 2026 | **Appeals, minimal version** (§4.12, DEC-057). **What can be appealed:** a committee's decline, or a park when the group was parked before ("parked twice"). The 15-working-day window opens only for those, after the preliminary review or after the presentation. A preliminary review's park or decline is now a decision on record (`decisions.round_id`), with its own decision file. **Filing:** the submitter or any co-contributor files once per decision from the new **Appeals** page (`/appeals`; message links lead there). They give grounds, a statement of up to 500 words and up to five web links. The links are stored as given and never fetched. They may also ask for a 10-minute hearing. Both personal-data checks run on the statement before anything is stored: the patterns, then the gatekeeper. If the gatekeeper is unavailable, the appeal waits rather than being stored unchecked. **Who hears it:** a LOB committee's decision goes to the whole core committee, minus anyone with a conflict. A core committee decision goes to a panel of three by rotation (fewest appeals heard first). The panel never includes anyone with a conflict on the group (the live §3.1 check) or the person who recorded the decision, and always includes at least one member who did not vote for the outcome. The most senior member chairs, taken as the longest on the committee because the HR export has no grade. If fewer than three qualify, every eligible member sits. **Outcomes** are recorded by the panel chair with at least 40 characters of reasons. Upheld returns the group to its outcome. Re-score sends it back to scoring, and the next round shows the panel's direction. Overturned sends a preliminary decision straight to an invitation, above the line; after a presentation, it returns the group to the chair to decide again, picked if capacity allows. The appeal file (`appeals/YYYY/APL-….md`, appellant as a contributor code, panel as a size) is linked from the decision file. `appeals/index.md` keeps live counts by deciding body and outcome, names removed. Messages `appeal.filed` (to the panel) and `appeal.decided` (to the colleagues) are new draft templates for HR. The not-shortlisted, not-accepted and not-presented drafts now say whether and until when the decision can be appealed. Behind a new switch, `appeals.filing` (off); the panel's decision is behind `committee.flow`. "Coming soon": appeals against review desk decisions (the day-one "Disagree with this?" stays) and against missed time limits, calendar invitations for the hearing, the full-committee re-hearing with the colleagues presenting, and the outside seat. Restoring points on an overturned appeal stays with the points owner (lane A), using `appeals.outcome` and the `appeal.decided` event | §4.12; DEC-057; non-negotiables 2, 6, 7 and 8 | Every park appealable; a panel for LOB decisions too | Yes |

| BLD-C015 | 11 Nov 2026 | **The isolated speech service** (round B of the verification review; completes BLD-C012). A `speech` container under the compose profile `speech`, so dev and CI do not start it unless asked. Its image holds the speech engine (faster-whisper 1.1.1, with IndicConformer still to come) and one standard-library file, and nothing of the worker. It runs as uid 10002 with a read-only root filesystem, a 64 MB tmpfs `/tmp`, `cap_drop: ALL`, `no-new-privileges`, a pids limit, a memory limit (3 GB), no env file, no published port and the model mounted read-only. It sits on the `speech` network (`internal: true`), which only the worker shares: no route to Postgres, the store, mail, the web app or the internet. The worker sends the recording over HTTP with `X-Speech-Token` (a shared internal token given only to the worker and the service; in UAT it lives in the database env file, never the app env file) and gets text back. The service caps a request at 6 MB, gives each recording a wall-clock limit and keeps the model loaded between notes. Inside it, the engine runs in a long-lived child with a cleared environment (no token), address-space, core and file limits; a child that overruns or dies is killed and restarted, and that note fails. The worker refuses a `fake` engine outside dev and test, and retries while the service is unreachable. The deploy starts it when `SPEECH_ENGINE_URL` is set and checks the token and the model folder. `make uat-rehearsal` does not start it, because there is no model there to load | A decoder exploit must reach nothing worth having: no secret of the platform, no database and no internet | A separate uid, seccomp or user namespaces inside the worker container | Yes |

A compose test reads `infra/docker-compose.yml` and the UAT overlay and checks: the profile, read-only, tmpfs, non-root, no capabilities, no ports, no env file, the allowed environment only, read-only volumes, the internal network shared only by the worker and the service, and the web app never getting the token. The speech service, which reaches the worker only over the speech network, meets a closed port. If a peer is set but no interface matches, the worker refuses to start (`service.api_bind_refused`): binding everywhere would quietly reopen it. Without a peer (host-side runs, tests) it listens everywhere as before. The server also takes the token out of its own environment once read. A test runs the real entry point and has the fake engine try `/proc/<parent>/environ`: "denied". A token in a mounted file was considered and not used: the child, as the same user, could read the file too. **Residual risk, accepted:** a kernel-level exploit from the engine could still reach the token. | BLD-C019 | 11 Nov 2026 | **No queue without end, and a non-executable /tmp** (round B review, important 2 and 3). The speech service's `/tmp` tmpfs is `size=64m,mode=1777,noexec,nosuid,nodev`. A recording waits at most 30 s (`LOCK_WAIT_S`) for the engine, then gets 503 `busy`. The worker treats `busy` (and `engine_not_ready`) as retryable, so a note that only waited is tried again rather than failed for good. After the wait, a recording whose client has gone (the connection closed) is not run. The worker's client timeout is the engine's limit plus the wait plus 15 s, always above the service's own | A long recording must not make every other note fail | A queue with a position, which is more than one service needs | Yes |

Dev and test may point it anywhere (the contract test uses localhost) | A recording must never be sent anywhere but the isolated service, and a sample sentence must never stand in for someone's words | Check only for the word "fake" | Yes |
| BLD-C031 | 11 Nov 2026 | **Speech service minors** (round B review). Content-Length must be ASCII digits (else 411; a value that does not parse is 400). Each request must arrive within 60 s in total (`READ_DEADLINE_S`, a timer that cuts the socket) on top of a 10 s per-read timeout, and at most 8 connections are open at once (a bounded semaphore; more get 503 `busy`). An engine answer over 1 MB is refused and the engine restarted. `_start` kills a child that hung or failed while starting. The child speaks on a duplicate of fd 1, and fd 1 itself goes to /dev/null before the model loads. The service needs a token of 32 or more characters (the dev default is 32). In dev and UAT compose the web app's `SPEECH_SERVICE_TOKEN` is set to empty, which wins over any `.env` value, and `.env.example` says not to put it there | Small ways to hold or confuse the service | — | Yes |

| BLD-C117 | 17 Nov 2026, amended 20 Nov 2026 (BLD-C200) | **Review fixes for voice through Sarvam** (two independent reviews of 679811d, no blocker). (1) A streaming session that cannot open is metered as a `route_error`, so an outage on the streaming path counts toward the automatic switch-off. (2) Only the service's failures count toward it: 401, 403, 429, 5xx, timeouts, unreachable, bad replies, broken streams and stream or speech errors; 400, 413 and 422 (bad or too short audio, a malformed field) are recorded as `bad_input` and never counted. (3) The check on text going to mayura or bulbul is the gateway's own leak check (any pattern hit, no domain allowed); text is first masked for the service (`mask_for_service`: an ARN, EUIN, IFSC or KFin address becomes kind + •••• + the last two characters too), so a distributor's ARN does not cost the English copy; if the check still refuses, the message keeps the person's words with no English copy, and Listen answers 503 and the player uses the on-device voice. Amended 20 Nov 2026 (BLD-C200): text for mayura is then also tagged by the gateway's own tagger and leak-checked on the tagged text (`gateway.sarvam.tag_for_service`), and its reply's tags are checked back (`tags_back`). (4) REST speech to text reads for 20 seconds at most; each Listen part has a 60-second deadline overall, not only per message. (5) Audio that is not base64 is `hosted_bad_reply`. (6) The go/no-go voice rows come from lane A's `core/speech_route` (`voice_problem`, `hosted_problem`), which include the approval check. (7) Listen's first, uncached play asks the Sarvam gate only on the Sarvam route, so dev's fake route plays without a key. (8) Listen is for ideas with the desk (`with_review_desk`, `needs_you`) only: `draft`, `checking`, `withdrawn` (BLD-E011) and `merged` are not found, and prefetch never touches `checking`. (9) The adapter's test fakes lane B's module as missing rather than relying on it. (10) One synthesis per (idea, script version, voice) under an advisory lock; the second caller is served from the cache. (11) Every part is masked and checked before the first call; prefetch asks the gate; a failed prefetch is remembered in `listen_failures` and that script and voice wait 6 hours. (13) `GET /api/voice/config` returns `idle_seconds`. The page downsamples from the context's own rate (44.1 or 48 kHz; a context forced to 16 kHz cannot take a microphone in Firefox) by averaging each output sample's window counted from the first sample, so there is no drift over 3 minutes, then writes 16-bit little-endian PCM with a DataView and cuts 32,000-byte chunks numbered from 0. The audio context is made inside the click (Safari), the microphone is asked for before `voice/start` (no stream is opened for someone who says no), and the source is connected only once the stream is open. One chunk is in flight at a time and the next waits; a chunk that fails on the network, 408, 429, 500, 502, 504 or 503 `unavailable` is sent again with the same seq after 0.5, 1.5 and 3 seconds (the worker answers a repeat without sending it twice). After that, or on 409 `chunk_out_of_order` or `stream_ending`, the mic goes off and the page polls `GET …/voice/:stream` every 2 seconds, offline or not, for up to VOICE_IDLE_SECONDS plus 5 minutes, until the worker has finished the note. Stop and the 180-second cap (counted from the samples sent, so exactly 180 chunks) send the last part second and `voice/end`, retried the same way; a worker answer that is already the note (the cap, idle, replaced) is taken as it is. An interruption (the context suspended or interrupted, the track ended) sends `end` once, then polls. Leaving the screen sends `end` with `keepalive` and the worker keeps the words. VOICE_NOTICE shows in idle until the person's first recording on this device (`localStorage` `1pc.voice.notice-seen`, read and written in try/catch; where storage is blocked it shows every time). Synthetic office sentences only; the speech-to-text calls hear the tts audio, decoded to 16 kHz mono s16le by macOS's built-in `afconvert` (no new dependency; list arguments, no shell, no key, a private temporary folder removed after; without it both fail with `no_decoder` and the summary says why). Listen's audio store and the drafts' stream store are stood in for in memory. Its own throwaway database (created, migrated with dbmate, dropped even on failure), `speech.hosted` on inside it. Per call: the reply parses as our code expects; masked, in two parts: the text the path hands on (to Sarvam, or back to its caller) is what the real masking function (`mask_for_service`, `leave_out_blocked`, `hide_text`) returned for that call (the check hands the path a fresh copy of each output, or where the path stores it a copy ending in a zero-width sentinel that stays in the check's own database and is never sent or printed, so a path that ignores the output fails), and before any network call each of those functions tags a synthetic vector from `pii.vectors`, which never leaves the process (amended 20 Nov 2026, BLD-C200: for translate, the text sent is what `gateway.sarvam.tag_for_service` returned for what `mask_for_service` returned, and before any network call the tagging step puts tags in place of a synthetic colleague's name, in `users` only inside a rolled-back transaction, and of a vector's values); the `speech_calls` row (call, model, outcome, `error_code` only a code with a prefix the gateway's own codes use, `ref_id` only an ID or uuid, no text in any column); for speech to text a word of each spoken sentence came back, and with `speech.hosted` off the call refuses before `sarvam._post` or `_ws_open` (both replaced by tripwires). Output: one line per call (call, model, HTTP status or ws close code, ms, `words: yes/no` for speech to text, PASS or FAIL with our own code) and a summary; never text, audio, the key, a header, a URL or a traceback; the worker's logs go to a buffer at INFO and are checked against the log rules; an uncaught exception, a settings error included, prints its type name only; SIGTERM and SIGHUP end the run like Ctrl-C, and the throwaway database is dropped, even after an interrupted CREATE. The check sets `SPEECH_MODE=sarvam` and `SPEECH_HOSTED_APPROVAL=DEC-108` for its own process, so a PASS does not test the deployed approval or mode settings; the go/no-go voice rows and the UAT smoke line do. It refuses without `SARVAM_API_KEY` (from the environment only, never `.env` or a command line, never printed in any form), when `CI` is set, and when `SARVAM_BASE_URL` is set. Run in the lead's own terminal with the stack up: `( set -a; . ~/.config/kfin/sarvam.env; set +a; make sarvam-live [ONLY=tts,…] )`; `SARVAM_LIVE_PYTHON` picks the Python (default `uv run --frozen python`). `tests/test_sarvam_live.py` runs the check itself against `tests/sarvam_fakes.py` with the decoder passed in, so CI (Linux) never needs `afconvert`. **The `sarvamai` SDK (PyPI and npm) is banned everywhere, the gateway included:** ruff `banned-api` (TID251) and `banned-module-level-imports` (TID253, which the gateway's TID251 ignore does not cover), and `scripts/ci/check_no_model_sdk.py` for any import and any listing in pyproject.toml, requirements*.txt or .in, uv.lock or package.json (`tests/test_c177_no_sarvam_sdk.py`). Docs check of 18 Nov 2026 (docs.sarvam.ai): no difference that breaks a call. **Live run, 19 Nov 2026, about 11:30 IST (the product lead, from `c-sarvam` with `c-merged`'s Python): 4 of 4 PASS** (tts bulbul:v4-flash ws 1000, 1494 ms; stt_rest saaras:v4 http 200, 622 ms, words yes; stt_stream saaras:v4 ws 1000, 5950 ms, words yes; translate mayura:v1 http 200, 878 ms; 13 log lines, IDs only). It settles the three ambiguous points, which stay as they are, with no client change before launch: the STT ws message `sample_rate` is the string "16000" (as the reference does), the TTS `speech_sample_rate` is the integer 24000 (as the SDK does), and one `final` per flush is enough (two sentences spoken before one flush, and a word of each came back on both speech-to-text calls). `SARVAM_TTS_SPEAKER` must be a bulbul:v4-flash persona ID (`name_lang_style`, such as `simran_en_customer`); a bulbul:v2 or v3 name such as `anushka` is refused by Sarvam. After launch: extend the `sarvamai` ban to Dockerfile `pip install` lines and to lockfiles other than uv.lock (package-lock.json). After launch: move stt_stream from `/speech-to-text/ws`, which Sarvam now documents as the generally-available legacy endpoint, to `/speech-to-text-realtime/ws`. | `gateway/sarvam.py` had only run against the fakes; a check through our own paths, with our masking, metering and switch, is the proof before the gates. Open: the `clusters_updated_at` trigger (`set_updated_at`, which stamps `now()` whatever the update sets) moves each cluster's `updated_at` on that write, and the worker role has no allowed way round it (no ALTER TABLE, no superuser setting). The one reader that orders by it, `views/wall.py` `_clusters`, is reached only through `/api/wall` and `/api/ideas`, which no screen calls, and lists only clusters with a repo file from before DEC-106. Batches of 32 commit as they go. Any stop, an error, Ctrl-C or `docker stop` (SIGTERM is turned into `Terminated` while it runs), writes `embeddings.rebuild_stopped` with the counts so far and the error type only, and the run is simply repeated until it exits 0. A finished run writes one `embeddings.rebuilt` row (actor `process:reembed`), with the mode and counts only; if that row cannot be written it logs the error type and exits 1. Output and logs carry the mode and counts only. After launch: chunks with empty text keep their old vector; the audit actor is a fixed string. | BLD-B272 | 20 Nov 2026 | **A reviewer's queued questions are kept between visits: S-R2's panel saves its queue as it changes, hidden before it is stored. Amends BLD-B244** (the product lead's P2 ask; S-R2.panel Asking, J-REVIEW/A1; L-QUESTIONS/4, L-QUESTIONS.states "queued (reviewer only)"; L-HIDE; non-negotiables 3, 4 and 6). (1) One worker write, `PUT /internal/desk/ideas/{ref}/question-sets/queued` (`desk.asking.save_queue`, the worker's `REVIEWERS`), behind `PUT /api/desk/ideas/{id}/question-sets/queued` (`work_review_queue`, the desk proxy's `writeRoute`; open while submissions are paused, as the desk's other writes, `UNGUARDED_WRITES`): the whole queue (texts, parts, suggested, the time to answer) replaces this reviewer's queued set and no one else's: one queued set per reviewer per idea (`question_sets_one_queued` on `(idea_uuid, by_user_id)`, migration 20261122200500, the fix round of 21 Nov), so a second reviewer's save never removes the first reviewer's queue. Audited (`desk.queue_questions`: IDs, counts, parts, the revision); nothing is logged. (2) Hiding first: each question goes through `adapters.hide` as Send's do (the patterns, then the gatekeeper), keyed by its row (`adapters.hide_queued`), and what hide kept for it (its `hidden_details` rows, and each vault value no other row of the idea holds) is dropped in the same transaction (lane C's `hide.forget`), because the sharer's idea page lists every `hidden_details` row of the idea (`ideapage.read.hidden`). "Hide this" also writes rows keyed by a queued question's id (lane C's sweep masks every question on the idea), so wherever lane B deletes a queued set (a save that replaces it, Send, a decision, a merge) `asking.drop_queued` first calls `adapters.forget_question` (lane C's `forget`, `source="desk"`) for each of its questions, in the same transaction and after lane C's lock: no `hidden_details` row outlives its question, and every vault value another row of the idea holds (the sharer's own words, a sent question) stays. Only the masked copy is stored, sealed as Send seals it (`questions.text_encrypted`), so the reviewer reads the masked copy after a reload; one sent back unchanged is kept as stored, with no second model call. At Send the questions go through `hide` again, as before. (3) Compare-and-swap, and no race with Send: a save carries `base`, the revision the page last knew was stored (the read's `queued.rev`, 0 when the reviewer has none, then the `rev` each kept save returns; never a clock), and `after`, the latest set the page knew (`panel.set_id` once answered, else null). The worker reads this reviewer's queued set FOR UPDATE: when its `queue_rev` (0 when none; `question_sets.queue_rev`, migration 20261122200400) is not `base` it answers 409 `queue_stale` and stores nothing; otherwise it replaces the set, stores `base + 1` and returns it. A `base` that is not a whole number with 0 ≤ base < 2^53 − 1 is 422 `base_invalid`. It also refuses with 409 and keeps nothing when the idea has left the desk (decided or merged: `already_decided`), or when the latest set sent is not `after` or is still waiting on answers (`queue_stale`). So a stale tab's save is refused, and that tab stops saving. Send, Decide and Merge drop every queued set on the idea, each reviewer's (`asking.drop_queued` in `asking.send_set`, `decide.send`, `merging.merge_locked`): once questions go, or the idea is decided or merged, no queue is wanted. (4) Only its reviewer reads it back: `reads._queued` reads by `by_user_id`, now with `rev`, and gives an emptied queue with no questions so its revision still counts; `asking._asked` (the suggestions) no longer reads another reviewer's queued set; every other `question_sets` reader in `desk/` already leaves `queued` out, and so do the sharer's, the conversation's, the Inbox's and comms' readers. (5) The panel (`lib/desk/panel.ts` `queueSaver`, `queuePut`; `reviewer-idea.tsx`): after a change to the queue (add, remove, re-time; typing a question or a reason is not one) it saves the whole queue 700 ms after the changes pause, one save at a time, and nothing while the worker holds the same queue; `base` moves only to a revision the worker returned. It pauses while Send or a decision is on its way and resumes if that fails; what still waits goes with `keepalive` when the page goes (`pagehide`, unmount), even beside a save in flight, then with `base + 1` (refused if that save fails, which is acceptable). On 409 `queue_stale` it stops saving for this page view: no retries and no words; the browser keeps the queue, Send still sends it, and a reload shows what is stored. Any other failed save keeps the browser's queue, shows nothing and is tried again on the next change. No new words. Tests: `test_1pc_desk_b272_queue_db.py` (a stored queue comes back; a PAN and a mobile number are stored masked and leave no `hidden_details` and no vault value; the sharer's own PAN and a sent question's number keep their rows and vault values when a queued question with the same value goes; after "Hide this", a replacing save, Send, Decide and Merge leave no row keyed by a deleted question; two tabs from one `base`: the second is 409 and the first is kept; `base` one off is 409; a second reviewer keeps the first's queue and Send drops both; a save after Send, Decide or Merge is refused; another reviewer, the committee and the sharer never read it), `test_1pc_desk_routes.py` "BLD-B272 … review desk only", `desk-panel.test.ts` (the BLD-B272 block: `base` only from returned revisions, no clock, a 409 stops saving, the close-time save beside one in flight sends `base + 1`), `rbac.test.ts`, e2e `review-desk.spec.ts` "BLD-B272 … still there after a reload" | The product lead: a reviewer's queued questions survive leaving and coming back, hidden on the stored text first. The fix round (21 Nov): the clock-based revision let a stale tab's save always win, and a second reviewer's save removed the first's queue; compare-and-swap on one set per reviewer makes "a stale tab is refused" true | Keep the queue in the browser's storage (one device only, and the words on the device unmasked); one queued set per idea, replaced by whoever saves (a second reviewer's save removed the first's queue); a revision that only grows from the browser's clock (a stale tab's save always won); retry a stale save from the stored revision (it would overwrite the other tab's newer queue) | Yes |

| BLD-D026 | 21 Nov 2026 | **C-ONES-FAN takes the Home board's motion, and several posts on a phone scroll natively with snap; the look at rest is unchanged** (C-ONES-FAN.behaviour/2, /4, /5, /6, /7, C-ONES-FAN.card; F-MOTION.rules/4–6, F-MOTION.reduced; `apps/web/src/components/ui/ones-fan.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/motion.css` section 5, the C-ONES-FAN section of `apps/web/src/app/components.css`). (1) **Where it lives:** every move is in `motion.css` under `(prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` (the fly-out, the hover and the side panel's exit also need `min-width: 641px`), so under reduced motion the fan has no transition or animation at all and takes each pose at once; `components.css` keeps the still poses and no animation, as BLD-D020's test pins it. (2) **The fly-out** (Home.dc.html `.ofan.opened .ocard`, `oFly`): on the first spread each card goes from its stacked pose to its row pose with `transform 0.8s cubic-bezier(.2,.85,.25,1)`, `filter 0.5s ease`, `opacity 0.45s ease`, delayed `--i × 45ms`, and arcs up 26px and 1.5deg at 40% (`translate` and `rotate`, so the arc adds to the glide); the cards the stack hides (the fifth on) fade in as they fly (`oShow`, 0.45s). The region carries `data-fly="open"` for 0.8s + (n − 1) × 45ms + 50ms, and `will-change: transform, filter` only then. Spread, the same 0.8s glide straightens the edge card as it comes up. The `.edge` and `.far` poses are rewritten in the stack's own list of functions (`translateX(0) perspective(1400px) translateX(0) translateZ(…) rotateY(…)`; `translateX(0)` is no move, so nothing at rest changes), so each glide interpolates function by function, not through a matrix. (3) **Hover on the stack**, only under `(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)`: the fan opens to 68px a card and −16deg (from 52px and −24deg) and the top card leans out (`perspective(1400px) translateZ(24px) rotateY(4deg)`), written as whole transforms so the stacked rule keeps its literals; BLD-D020's follow-the-pointer tilt now also needs that query (`FAN_FINE`), never a finger, never under reduced motion. (4) **The side panel** stays mounted 0.4s with `data-leaving`, inert and `aria-hidden` at once (so it leaves the reading order on the spread and focus still moves on to Next, WCAG 2.4.3), absolutely placed in the column it sat in (left where it is when the container query put it under the stack), and takes the board's exit: opacity 0.25s, `translate: 32px 0` 0.4s, visibility at 0.4s; then it unmounts. Without motion it goes at once, as before. (5) **Cheer:** `OnesPost` adds `.on` only when the person's own press turns Cheer on; `button.opost-cheer.on svg` runs `cheerPop` (0.42s, `cubic-bezier(.34,1.56,.64,1)`, scale 1.35 at 40%), so a cheer already given on load never pops; the filled heart stays the pressed state's own. (6) **The phone row** (640px and under, several posts): the view is the row's own scroll (`overflow-x: auto`, `scroll-snap-type: x mandatory`, `scroll-snap-align: start`, `overscroll-behavior-x: contain`, `touch-action: auto`), replacing the JS one-card-per-swipe track; the track's `--at` transform applies only above 640px. The current card is set once per settle, from where the row rests (the card whose snap point is nearest the scroll position; snap points come from the cards' `offsetLeft`, which ignores transforms, so a track still gliding back from the desktop's `translateX` never skews them), on `scrollend`, or after 120ms without a scroll where a browser has no `scrollend`; so "Post N of M", the one card in the reading order and Previous and Next stay in step, and the polite line changes once per settle (F-MOTION.focus/3). Previous, Next, ← → and the wheel scroll the view to the card's snap point (`smooth`; `auto` unless motion is allowed outright, where the CSS also sets `scroll-behavior: auto` under reduced motion, and then the line changes at once and the settle agrees). While such a scroll runs, its target is kept and the next press goes one card on from it, so a quick second Next goes two cards on; a pointer or a finger on the row, or a wheel step the browser scrolls itself, clears the target, and the settle reads where the person left the row. On a phone the first wheel step moves a card, as there is no stack; the pointer swipe handler leaves a finger to the native scroll; turned to a phone's width, the row jumps to the card the line names one animation frame later (once layout has the phone's rules), and if nothing scrolled the line follows the card the row rests on, so the card on screen is never the inert one; turned wider, a scroll still heading somewhere is dropped, and Previous, Next and the wheel count from the target only while the phone's width holds. At 640px and under `.fan-track` has `transition: none` (the phone's media rules only; the desktop glide and the look at rest are unchanged), so narrowing drops the desktop's `translateX` at once. (6a) **Focus:** before the current card changes, if focus is inside the card that leaves the reading order, it moves on to Next, or to Previous at the last card (WCAG 2.4.3). (6b) The JS moves (the row's glide, the tilt) run only where `(prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` holds, as the CSS's do, so a browser that matches neither query stays still too. BLD-D020's `.fan-track` and `.fan-card > *` transitions keep their place (BLD-D020's still-section test pins them; the reduce block turns them off). (7) Unchanged: `spreadOut()` and focus never on an inert card, the wheel's hand-back at either end, the scrim and glows, the names, BLD-D020's look at rest. Closing back to the stack (`oFly2`) is not built: the fan has no way back to the stack (C-ONES-FAN.behaviour names none), so it has nothing to run on. (2) `submit/own.py` `find(lock=True)` locks hide first, reading an IDEA- reference to its uuid with no lock: `edit.save`, the fix sheet (`fix.store`, `mask_text`), the helpers' words, sorting and Submit. (3) `submit/check.py` `run` locks hide before the idea's row; its final pass reaches hide through `mask_text`. Lane F's review listed this path as "no mixed order", but it was reversed (the row, then hide). (4) `attachments/files.py`: `_owner` (a draft by its uuid; a shared idea and an answer, each read to the idea's uuid with no lock) and `_conversation_owner` lock hide, then the idea's or the answer's row as before, for `accept` and `accept_link` (`hide_name`); an answer's row was locked before hide too (`FOR UPDATE OF an`). The file check's finish (`check`) locks hide before the file's row (`hide_file`). A row gone between the read and the row lock answers as before. A known cost: for these callers a `hide_message` holds hide's lock across the gatekeeper's model call, which BLD-C067 otherwise avoids. A draft's own writes already held the draft's row across that call. Now a team-up ask or answer on the same draft waits for it too. With `ai.all` off on the launch server there is no model call. The rule, fixed with lane A (BLD-A239 asserts it): `jobs/runner.py` `JobWaits(seconds, code)`; on it `run_one` sets the job `queued`, `attempts = attempts - 1` (the claim's +1 undone), `run_at = now() + seconds`, `last_error = code`, `locked_by = NULL` (`locked_at` as the claim set it), logs `jobs.waiting` (`job_id`, `job_type`, `code`), never makes the job `dead` and never runs the dead handler. `attachments/files.py` `check`: first, before the virus scan, if either switch is off (read past the 10-second cache), `checks.waiting_for = 'gatekeeper'`, the file stays `checking`, and `JobWaits(300, 'waiting_gatekeeper')`, so no scan is repeated while it is off. A late `GatekeeperWaits` with the gateway's `switched_off` or `daily_budget` waits the same way; any other gateway failure (a refusal, an answer off its schema, a timeout) stays `CheckWaits("gatekeeper")`, a counted try with today's backoff (the coordinator's narrowing, 22 Nov). Every `CheckWaits` keeps its reason in `checks.waiting_for` (scanner, store, ocr, gatekeeper); a check that really runs clears it; a file that gives up (`check_failed`) keeps the wait it died of, or `none`, so every such row from this build has the key. The row's line while it waits: `problem_line` gives "Waiting for the personal details check. It runs when the assistant is back." for a checking file waiting for the gatekeeper, in the FileItem and the draft read, and `check/model.ts` shows it in place of "Checking for personal details" (the Share a problem box's file row and Check it's Proof share `rowView`). Who may see and open a file is unchanged: a waiting file is seen only by the person who added it and opened by no one | Mode B (`ai.all` off) is how the launch server runs, and UAT.md promises that scans queue there: files must wait safely, not fail. A wait is not a failure, so it must not use the tries real failures need | A separate runner role for `file.check` (after launch); a longer backoff only (still fails, only later); skipping the gatekeeper while it is off (refused: non-negotiable 4) | Yes |
